# Plan: keep the Mac awake while AI works

- Status: a plan, not started. Nothing in it is implemented. First written on 2026-10-06 for Claude Code only, and revised the same day after the owner's answers: Awake should do this "for all the current mainstream AIs such as Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and Gemini"; the timings stay; the 12-hour cap goes, and an automatic session has the same 365-day maximum as any session; what the user decides always overrides the automatics; and the owner asked what a stop by the user should do (B-5). Then revised once more after two reviews of that revision, whose points are applied or answered (see "How this plan was checked"); they added question B-14. Revised a third time the same day after the owner's answers to that revision: the name is "AI" (S-3); only agents and surfaces shown to work with Awake are offered (S-4, the inclusion rule); a session the user started by time is a minimum while AI works, which keeps it going past its end until the work ends (B-4, B-14, 5.9); every automatic session must end in any imaginable situation, which a renewable lease that the root helper's own timer ends now guarantees, whatever happens to the watcher (B-12, 5.14); and every Cursor, Codex and Gemini fact was checked against Adrafinil's code and the vendors' GitHub sources (section 2). Finished the same day after two reviews of that third revision, whose points are applied or answered (see "How this plan was checked"): renewals and ends run as their own short processes under time limits, a clock set back cannot make old events look new, an agent that never stops is bounded by a presence limit (new question B-18), a change of sessions with the lid closed can no longer leave the Mac awake with no session, and only surfaces with a preflight step are offered; they also added question B-19. Then B-6 took up the owner's question about sounds and notifications: automatic sessions are silent by default, guardrail ends and problems are always told, and one opt-in switch makes them follow the user's own settings (M8). The owner agreed to the recommendations for B-15 to B-19 the same day, and then to all the remaining ones, so every question is answered. Answered questions are marked as such below. The open ones follow the recommended answer until the owner decides. A Mac preflight (section 10) comes before any code: about 70 minutes for the owner's own setup (Cursor with the Claude Code extension, Cursor's own agent, and `claude` in Cursor's terminal), about 25 for Claude Code in a terminal, headless and in the Desktop app, about 15 for the lease with today's Awake (10.6), plus about 20 minutes for each of Codex and Gemini CLI, which ship only once their preflight has passed. It needs no Awake build, only the installed 2.4.0, the agents and a test hook.
- In brief: once the user turns it on (`awake --ai-hooks on`, or `Keep awake while AI works` in the menu), each supported agent's own hooks append one line per event to a file in the user's runtime folder. Four adapters write those hooks: Claude Code (a terminal, also an editor's integrated terminal, headless `claude -p`, the VS Code and Cursor extension, the Desktop app), Codex (terminal and `codex exec`), Cursor's own agent (Agent chat, Cmd+K and the interactive `agent` CLI) and Gemini CLI (terminal, `gemini -p`, ACP). Each surface is listed as supported only after its own step of the Mac preflight has shown it working with Awake (S-4); Codex's IDE extension and app, Cursor's Agents Window and programs that host Claude Code with the user's settings (Zed's Claude agent), for example, wait for that. One agent-neutral core reads the lines: a small watcher keeps a "holder" for each agent conversation and subagent that works, waits for the user, or has background work. While any holder needs it, the watcher runs an ordinary Awake session tied to its own PID (`-w`), lid-closed in password-free mode, as a lease: a 2-minute session that the watcher renews about every 30 s with the helper's existing `extend`. About a minute after the last holder ends, it ends that session the way `awake --` does, so a closed Mac sleeps. For Claude Code it first asks once whether that session is still busy (`claude agents --json`). If anything goes wrong, the end does not depend on the watcher: when nothing renews the session, whether the watcher died, hung, froze or went wrong, its log was lost or the disk filled up, the root helper's own timer ends it within 2 minutes, as it ends any timed session, and with the lid closed the Mac sleeps. Each renewal runs as its own short `awake` process under a time limit, so one that hangs is killed and holds no lock. Hooks that fall silent end their holders by the time rules, within 15 minutes, or at most 2 hours, after which the renewals stop; an agent that itself never stops (a tight loop) is stopped by the presence limit, as nothing is renewed once nobody has used the Mac for 24 hours (B-18, recommended; 5.14). Whatever the user does wins: a start by hand replaces an automatic session, a stop pauses automatic sessions until the user's next prompt typed at the Mac, a changed setting applies to the running automatic session within 30 s, and the automatics never shorten, end or replace a session the user started. A session the user started by time is a minimum: while AI works it goes on past its end, and it then ends 90 to 120 s after the work does (5.9). The helper does not change (protocol 9), and nothing changes for users who never turn it on.
- Target version: after 2.4.0, the next minor version: 2.5.0. 2.4.0 is in its Mac QA on `dev` now (plan-2.4.0.md:3, faster-start-stop-2.md:3). The feature adds `### Added` entries, so `tools/release.sh` suggests a minor release (tools/release.sh:183-191). The helper does not change: no new command, no new file, not one byte. So the helper protocol stays 9 (bin/awake:172, awake-helper:56), and nobody is asked for a password at the update.
- Written: 2026-10-06, against `dev` at `a9cd6d1`, from the owner's request of that day: "I was not aware of Adrafinil; we should study what they do with AI agents at some point. It would be a nice addition to be able to select 'keep awake as long as Claude works'." Revised the same day against `dev` at `1bbb06f`, whose code is that of `a9cd6d1` (`git diff a9cd6d1 1bbb06f` touches only this plan), from the owner's answers quoted in "Questions for the owner", and again against `dev` at `f6f79f1`, whose code is still that of `a9cd6d1` (`git diff a9cd6d1 f6f79f1` touches only this plan), from the owner's second set of answers of that day. Bare line numbers are lines of `bin/awake` at that code. Other files are named, for example `awake-helper:851` or `StatusBarController.swift:603`. The agents' sources are cited as "How this plan was checked" describes: `<page>.md:LINE` for Claude Code's docs, `codex-rs/…` for Codex's source, `packages/…` for Gemini CLI's, and `[CD]`, `[FL]` and `[RO]` for a mirror of Cursor's docs and two projects that recorded Cursor's hooks running.
- Words used in this plan: an **automatic session** is a session that Awake starts by itself because an AI agent works; a session **started by hand** is any other (the CLI, the picker, the app, or a command that some program runs). An **adapter** is one agent's part: its hook file, its install and removal, how its events map to the common verbs, and its limits. The **lease** is how an automatic session is kept: a short end that the watcher keeps moving later while work goes on. The **floor** is the same renewal applied to a session the user started by time, past its own end, while AI works. 5.1 has the rest.
